A Feather for the Prince","volume":"婚后外传：不再坠落的马戏女王","anchor":"chapter-0190","paragraphs":["The circus offered Mara a crown, a white horse and a villain to defeat before supper. Cassian was offered a feathered hat. His dissatisfaction was immediate. The impersonal cast sheet called him PRINCE B. \"I feel my role lacks depth.\"","\"It has considerable height,\" she said, adjusting the feather.","Mara would not meddle with the casting. They had come to watch a rehearsal, but Ari, the leading performer, had sprained her wrist. Fresh from surgical follow-up, the former acrobat could still direct the show; she could not carry the ceremonial sword. She asked Mara to ride across the ring and hand it to the comic prince. \"On horseback,\" Cassian repeated. \"Presumptively, the horse has read the script?\"","Ari laughed, then winced. The expectancy had been easier before anybody arrived. Her enjoyment had a hesitancy beneath it. She had returned to performing after a serious fall, and tonight's new act mattered to her. A stab of envy crossed her face when Mara mounted so easily. She looked ashamed of it. Mara was involved in the opening scene now. She recognised the self-consciousness and said nothing. Sympathy could become intrusive when it announced everything it noticed.","Tomas, Ari's partner, checked the low-density foam mats beneath the aerial rig. A high-functioning stage crew prepared lights while he inspected the equipment. He was an engineer fresh from a design course, but circus work had forced him into new intellectual territories. A humanistic understanding of fear mattered alongside calculations. Cloud computing managed the ticket system; it could not tell him why his partner froze beneath a silent rope.","Ari's father had once controlled the troupe. His patriarchal authority had gone unchallenged until she reported his violence. He was now in prison. That had ended his command, not her anguish. She could recollect his scorn more quickly than she could remember a teacher's admiration. An unfavorable review had hurt less than the words of someone she loved. His old insistence that she was incompetent had survived the end of his career. Tomas had never treated her injury as insignificant, though other people thought she ought to be grateful and merry by now.","The circus kept a quiet paddock outside the ring. Mara watched the group-living horses settle when their companions came closer. A solitary animal had been calling anxiously. The trainer would slow down its rehearsal rather than force a dramatic entrance. \"Whichever route we choose,\" Ari said, \"we need a calm horse.\"","Ari watched with eagerness and a touch of dread. Mara wondered whether Ari could hear how naturally she offered an animal the compassion she denied herself.","The first run went badly. A comic prince missed his entrance; a recorded fanfare began too soon. The cues had been handled incompetently, and the ensuing fright spread through the cast. Ari dropped her clipboard. Her horror was not superficial. She had heard the crash as another fall. Tomas switched off the sound and left the rig undisturbed. The alleviation came when everyone stopped asking her to explain herself at once.","Cassian removed the hat. \"I believe I have frightened the orchestra with this feather.\"","Ari managed a smile. She did not want every conversation to turn into therapy, and his absurd earnestness gave her a route back to ordinary company. Her discontent with the show was partly technical. Her sorrow belonged elsewhere. Both could be real without every mistake becoming a revelation about her past.","At lunch, she admitted that waiting between attempts was torture. Fulfillment had once meant a perfect leap and the audience's awe. Now she wanted to remain in the room without her hands beginning to quiver. Tomas listened with empathy, resisting the urge to make a cheerful plan before she finished. He could help liberate the act from its old form, but she had to decide whether she wanted that.","Rowan arrived, ready to explode with laughter at the feather. He carried sandwiches and his usual partiality toward expensive mustard. He offered to fund an extra rehearsal day. Ari was wary of favours; her father's generosity had always carried a debt. \"I favour a show I can enjoy without worrying about you,\" Rowan said. \"Thereafter I expect nothing beyond a ticket.\"","She believed him just enough to eat.","A retired costume maker watched with distaste when an assistant called Ari oversensitive. \"Sensitiveness isn't permission to mock someone,\" she said. \"And it doesn't mean every disagreement is harassment. Tell her what happened. Don't tell her what she is.\"","Tomas did not disguise his disgust at the careless joke. He would aim to keep up with the changes she requested. Ari asked the assistant to reconsider the cue timing. He apologised, not grandly but specifically. The apology did not dissolve her resentment. It made the next exchange possible. For a woman accustomed to defending herself against scorn, a plain correction could carry unexpected emotion.","Outside, children crowded around a painted dragon. Cassian regarded it with mock terror. Mara challenged him to pat its nose. He complied, then caught her around the waist when the foam head swung toward them. Her delighted shriek was mostly excitement. The energetic children followed with zeal. His ego survived the audience's laughter with difficulty.","Ari watched them with nostalgia for the carefree days she was never certain she had actually lived. The future could be different without making the old days innocent. Sadly, she knew that. She would not let the old hatred deceive her into expecting cruelty everywhere. The well-educated engineer could still be impolite when frightened; she wanted him to notice. She would not let spite decide what happened next.","She asked to try the scene again. The captain of the crew was in charge, the mats were in place, and the music had been corrected. The performer most likely to surprise herself was standing beneath the rope. She was most likely to win the room by speaking honestly, not by ignoring the agony in her wrist. She was not ready to climb. She was ready to speak the first line, which today was a considerable thrill."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1010,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0190\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：不再坠落的马戏女王</div><h1>1. The Queen Changes the Ending","volume":"婚后外传：不再坠落的马戏女王","anchor":"chapter-0191","paragraphs":["Ari's first line came out too softly. The second carried to the back row. Mara, waiting on horseback at the opposite gate, watched the change with admiration. A voice could cross a ring without a leap. Ari's enjoyment was fragile, but it was her own. Tomas smiled with an earnestness that made her look away before she could begin to cry.","Then a child screamed outside the curtain. The horse lifted its head, and Mara felt the first quiver through its neck. She asked the trainer to slow down the movement around them. Group-living animals took cues from one another; the companion horse was already calm. The trainer brought it alongside rather than shout. Mara dismounted when directed and handed back the rein.","A stage assistant had started the dragon's loud recording to amuse a visitor. The child, who had expected a silent model, was in terror behind a bench. She did not meddle in the trainer’s handling of the horse. Ari crossed the ring before anyone else. She knelt a few feet away and waited. \"It gave me a fright too,\" she said. \"That was an impressive roar.\"","The horror in his expression sharpened her own emotion. Her empathy reached the boy where a cheerful command had failed. The sound stopped. The dragon's keeper showed him the hidden speaker only when he wanted to look. Alleviation came in steps: silence, distance, then curiosity. The incident had been handled incompetently, but nobody needed to pretend the child was insignificant because the danger had been imaginary.","Mara found Cassian beside the curtain. He had a leaf in his hair from the paddock and an expression of barely restrained rage. An assistant had dismissed the boy as a nuisance. \"Presumptively harmless entertainment,\" he said, \"still requires somebody to notice the audience.\"","\"You may remove the feather before intimidating anybody.\"","\"I already have. My full authority is restored.\"","She laughed against his shoulder, and the worst of his anger softened.","Ari’s distaste for that amusement needed no apology. The interruption brought Ari's own dread rushing back. Her father had called fear disgusting, then used it to control her. He was in prison now, but the patriarchal habits of the troupe had not vanished when his wagon left. Some people still expected Tomas to decide which act she could perform. Fear of renewed harassment had kept her quiet. Now she spoke openly. Her dissatisfaction sharpened into a useful request: he must stop answering questions addressed to her, even when he thought he was helping.","He looked stricken, then nodded. To his dismay, the impersonal words he meant kindly had sounded like another command. Compassion did not make him immune to a mistake. The stab of guilt would not help if it became a demand for her reassurance. He asked what she wanted to change. Tomas would scrutinize it with her. Ari pointed to the running order. She would favour a less rigid ending. The floor scene could remain undisturbed while they tried the dialogue. The old aerial climax was not the only ending. They could liberate the story from the fall that had once made it famous.","Tomas and the crew returned to the low-density mats. This high-functioning team could adapt if she gave them time. They would aim to turn the rope ascent into a floor scene, using shadow and voice rather than a stunt. A humanistic reading of the character made more sense than an impersonal demand for height. The villain's punishment did not need another body descending through darkness.","Mara could see Ari's hesitancy while she explained. Her ego had been tied to what she could do in the air. An intrusive memory crossed her mind, then receded. To change the act felt like admitting defeat. Nostalgia made the old version beautiful; sorrow made every lost skill look irreplaceable. Fulfillment might have to acquire a new shape, and that possibility frightened her almost as much as failure.","Their energetic crew was most likely to produce a good show if it could keep up with her revisions. She would pray for good timing rather than an absent fear. The costume maker called her courageous, then accepted her request for fewer compliments. The costume maker cut the new cape with surgical precision. She had worked through Ari's surgical recovery too, altering clothes so an injured shoulder could move comfortably. Her occasional scorn was reserved for people who called that adaptation a tragedy. \"A good costume fits the body that's here,\" she said. \"The body is not employed to justify my scissors.\"","Ari laughed. Thereafter, she spoke more freely. She could recollect the old staging without obeying it. Sadly, some former colleagues would call her afraid. They could keep their superficial judgement. The most likely people to enjoy the new ending were those who had never seen the first version anyway.","Nobody would let the old assistant’s scorn go unchallenged, or excuse spite as harmless teasing. Mara asked a well-educated visitor to stop trying to deceive the child with another roar. Rowan helped the ticket volunteer sift duplicate entries. Cloud computing had copied one promotion twice, causing understandable discontent among visitors who thought they held the same seats. The correction involved numbers and apologies, not accusations of deceit. These were intellectual territories far from Ari's rope, though her own stage worked better when someone else handled them.","She confessed a sudden envy of Mara's apparent ease. Mara told her about the first time she had frozen before a difficult climb. Cassian's face changed; he had not known that story. She did not turn it into an uplifting lesson. She had been terrified, had climbed down and had returned later by a different route. Her courage did not require lying about the first day.","Her self-consciousness could not be wiped away by a new costume. Ari's sensitiveness remained. So did her partiality for dramatic endings. The earlier expectancy returned without the same agony. Waiting could be torture, but rushing was worse. She would not meddle with every lighting cue to feel safe. She began to relish the thought of rewriting this one. \"Whichever version we choose,\" she said, \"the queen should win by being clever.\"","Mara approved with great passion. Cassian said he had experience of that arrangement. The prince's exaggerated anguish made the whole company merry. →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"x032c03","title":"3. No Fall Required","volume":"婚后外传：不再坠落的马戏女王","anchor":"chapter-0192","paragraphs":["On opening night, Ari entered the ring on horseback beneath a shower of paper stars. She wore a crimson cape and carried no sword. Mara had handed that duty back with theatrical sorrow. Cassian still wore the feather, having been informed that artistic consistency mattered more than personal dignity. He accepted the argument with the enjoyment of a man planning revenge in the form of a dance.","They had checked that the cues were in place. The new queen dismounted at the marked place. The trainer led the horse to its companion, respecting the needs of a group-living animal before chasing another picturesque moment. The ring stayed quiet until they were clear. Ari could feel her hands quiver. Her hesitancy did not prevent the first line. Neither did the stab of fear when she looked toward the silent rope.","The audience had come with expectancy, nostalgia and a few doubts. A former colleague had said the revised act was presumptively a lesser version. He had not watched it. Ari let his scorn remain outside the circle of light. The voice that used to tell her she was insignificant belonged to a man in prison. She could recollect it without giving it tonight's microphone.","Her scene began with a prince offering to save her. She declined. The response delighted the children and troubled the prince's ego. Cassian delivered his wounded line so grandly that Mara nearly missed her cue. She had a small role as the queen's disreputable friend, which several people considered perfect casting. Her bow expressed a deep satisfaction with that judgement.","Behind them, the high-functioning crew moved the shadow screens into place. Low-density foam walls made an enormous palace that could fall harmlessly in rehearsal; tonight nobody touched them without a cue. The aerial rig remained undisturbed. The performance had not been assembled incompetently merely because it used less machinery. A humane, humanistic story could create awe with a voice, a shadow and a woman refusing to kneel.","Ari played the old queen's anguish without imitating her own past. The distinction mattered. A fictional betrayal could carry horror and resentment without forcing her to relive every injury for an audience. Her sensitiveness to small changes in a scene had become useful. She heard the room's laughter settle and waited. Then her next line reached the farthest row.","The villain told her that she should favour obedience over freedom. He promised the alleviation of every difficulty if she surrendered the crown. She smiled with sweet partiality toward her own answer. \"A comfortable cage remains a cage.\"","The children cheered. Mara thought of how often one plain sentence could sound new when a person was ready to hear it. She also thought that a playwright deserved credit for choosing a good line. She had no need to turn every scene into her own life.","Ari's dissatisfaction with the old patriarchal ending had driven the rewrite. Now the queen persuaded the guards to leave their posts instead of fighting them. To liberate the palace, she opened its doors and let ordinary people enter. The low-density walls glowed with silhouettes. There was no fall, no scream, no body offered as the price of a thrill. The excitement came from wondering what she would do next.","Tomas was most likely to forget the time, despite his effort to keep up with her lightness. Tomas watched from the wing with empathy and obvious pride. Ari saw him once and had to look away. The fulfillment was not pure happiness. There was sorrow for the years she had lost, envy of the uninjured body she remembered and a deep, fierce enjoyment of the one carrying her through this scene. Her eagerness to return tomorrow surprised her. She did not need one feeling to defeat the others before taking her bow.","The seriousness of the final speech gave way to comedy. The curtain call nearly reduced Cassian to ruin. Mara caught his feather on her crown; they had to stand very still while the costume maker separated them. The audience assumed it was deliberate and roared. Ari laughed with a merry abandon that made her partner close his eyes for a moment. The return of his feather caused fresh dismay. Waiting had been torture earlier. This was worth the wait.","Afterward, a reporter asked whether the revised show was an admission that Ari could no longer perform. Tomas did not answer for her. The small change was noticeable. \"It's a performance,\" she said. \"You have just seen it.\"","His admiration carried no demand that she thank him for being decent. He offered her water. She took it, then leaned against him while Mara steered the crowd toward the refreshments. He was fresh from a breathless run around the ring and would reconsider the heavy costume before tomorrow. An impersonal publicity machine would have demanded more. An occasional unwelcome question could be handled without allowing intrusive attention to swallow the evening.","A superficial account might turn her aversion to the old act into an outrage against tradition. She did not owe it obedience. Ari would not describe the old years as a necessary lesson. Sadly, abuse had not made her an artist. Training, zeal and practice had done that. Her rage and disgust at what happened were not impolite. Forgiveness was not an entrance fee to enjoyment. She could sift the past without turning sadness into a permanent role. She could stop carrying hatred into every rehearsal without inventing affection for the man who hurt her. His place in prison and her place on the stage belonged to different stories now.","She would pray for no grand miracle. An ordinary rehearsal could offer fulfilment too. Mara found Cassian at the refreshment table, wearing his feather with a new reluctance to remove it. \"The child in the second row called me magnificent,\" he said.","\"Did you pay him?\"","\"Only in biscuits.\"","She pulled him into a corner where the lanterns softened his face. His earnest pride in a ridiculous evening filled her with affection. She kissed him slowly, then let him keep his promise of a dance. The music was quiet enough to hear him laugh.","Across the room, Ari rested her wrist and watched her company celebrate. The fright was over for tonight. Her relish for the stage had survived. Tomorrow might bring discontent, pain or another rehearsal that did not work. Whichever came, she could ask the room to slow down. Thereafter she would decide the next step, with people who listened beside her."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1088,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0192\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：不再坠落的马戏女王</div><h1>3. The Tragic Hamburger","volume":"婚后外传：公爵烧坏了午饭","anchor":"chapter-0193","paragraphs":["Cassian had survived interrogations, pursuit and marriage to Mara Vale. A hamburger almost defeated him. He turned it too early, lost half the meat to the grill and watched a small boy study the result with intense pity. \"You could make it a salad,\" the child suggested.","Mara kissed flour from her husband's cheek. \"An intuitive solution.\"","They were aboard a river steamer past fields of hay for a sight-seeing afternoon that had become a cooking competition. Rowan had signed them up, incurably convinced that embarrassment improved friendship. A professional chef named Flint waited opposite them with a perfect apron and a sly smile. His barbecue station gleamed. Cassian's contained a blackened fragment of bacon and a whopping amount of misplaced confidence.","The supposedly well-orchestrated introduction had already made a hat airborne. Cassian was susceptible to ridiculous headwear. The first round was simple: a warm lunch from a sealed basket. Mara found a packet of pea seeds and briefly suspected sabotage before discovering that the actual peas were in the cold box. The seeds were a gift from the garden sponsor. She set them aside. Flint's junior cook, Ess, laughed and pointed her toward the saucepan. Ess had worked a low-wage kitchen job for years; she could rescue a meal without insulting the person who had ruined it.","\"I insist on doing the sauce,\" Cassian said.","The first taste was bitter enough to make him blink. Mara gave him the vinegar. \"I insist on tasting it before the judges do.\"","The chef's station was serving a mutton skewer with a crisp garnish. Their basket held pork for the main dish, and Cassian kept looking wistfully at the steak on the neighbouring board. He had planned one impressive dinner, not several small portions. \"Don't liken the contest to a siege,\" Mara warned. \"We can feed people without conquering the kitchen.\"","A cordial host offered guests beer or a soft beverage. Mara chose grape juice. Cassian took water because he was still handling the grill. Behind them the bar prepared a cocktail with whiskey, while a donated bottle of whisky sat beside the winner's ribbon. The bar kept other liquor locked away from the busy service route. The wine had been set aside for the evening tables. Nobody needed to drink alcohol to take part, though Rowan's expression suggested that he considered this a missed opportunity.","A pre-teen guest named Remy carried two pet mice in a secure travel box. His mother kept him on the passenger deck, away from food preparation. The little animals had been at a school exhibition and were heading home after the trip. One was busy trying to chew a cardboard tube. Cassian regarded it mournfully. \"At least someone knows what to do with lunch.\"","Mara gave him a piece of melon. \"Chew this. Think less.\"","Flint overheard and offered a sarcastic comment about aristocratic incompetence. Ess quietly moved a hot handle away from a passing volunteer. Her practical talent was undoubted. The applause, however, went to Flint when he raised an unnecessary flame over his pan. Superficially, he looked like the most capable person aboard. Mara was not prepared to confuse spectacle with the whole job.","The judges arrived while her sauce still tasted sharply of vinegar. She added the planned ingredients slowly, asking Ess whether the heat was too high. Ess answered without taking over. Mara could reconcile her competitive pride with accepting help. Their dish would pretty much have been inedible without it, and she was not going to pretend otherwise.","A guest in a patriotic hat wanted to dedicate the event to the city's anniversary. The host agreed, provided he moved away from the preparation table. From this round onwards, the service route needed to stay clear. Thereby a speech about communal pride became, mercifully, a short one. Mara offered the man a piece of melon and reclaimed her work surface.","At the far end, an energy sector manager complained about the cost of fuel. The skipper explained that record-high bills had reduced this year's sailings. The festival helped keep the boat afloat financially, not merely in the literal sense. That gave the cooking competition a practical purpose beyond publicly humiliating Cassian. He remained unhappy about the hamburger on that account, though not about helping.","A guest’s diet excluded meat. Lunch needed to accommodate dietary requests. Mara made a bean dish separate from the meat, using the marked utensils. A bowl of tomato sauce was kept separate; it was likely to spill if balanced above the bread. A dietary note was information, not an invitation to debate someone's life. The brittle pastry lids stayed away from the steam until service. The decorations for a later trifle were edible, though he first mistook them for polished stones. Cassian carried chocolate decorations with a concentration usually reserved for secret correspondence. She rewarded him with a quick kiss that nearly made him drop the tray.","\"A self-serving incentive,\" Rowan observed.","\"A delicious one,\" Cassian replied.","The first judging went to Flint by one point. He looked pleased enough to be painted. Mara was intensely interested in the second round. Its theme was a medieval feast, adapted for a very small kitchen. The basket included mutton and pork again, quantities carefully measured so nobody would waste a whole joint. The old recipe was not unchangeable; Ess showed how to adapt it for the boat. A cloud passed over the sun, but the deck remained warm.","Then the skipper announced that a jammed service hatch would delay the next round. Flint's assistant had been caught on the other side with the chilled ingredients. Cassian looked from the narrow hatch to his wife. Mara was already unpinning her hair."],"questions":[],"wordCount":950,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0193\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：公爵烧坏了午饭</div><h1>1. Low-wage work had taught her to swallow annoyance. She could not reconcile her love of cooking with Flint's appetite for taking every good result. His sarcastic joke about her salary was the last thing she needed while carrying a hot saucepan. Mara asked him to move and let her through.","A piece of bacon had crisped too far while they waited. Cassian started again, then tested a sausage on the cooler part of the grill. The second round began under a cloudy sky. The menu's medieval theme was less a history lesson than a challenge to serve something warming. Cassian examined the mutton, then the pork, and begged for a steak that required fewer decisions. Mara gave him the vegetables instead. \"I insist on doing one thing well,\" he said.","\"Then start with not feeding the river.\"","The hamburger from the first round had broken in two again. He watched its final collapse mournfully before placing it in the proper waste container. It had not touched the water. The undoubted winner in the household would remain Mara's cook, who was not here to rescue them. Cassian, incurably hopeful, began a fresh patty for a guest who had requested one.","Mara made a pea purée while Ess prepared a crisp garnish. They were rivals, but neither saw an advantage in letting a guest eat an avoidable mistake. Ess showed her where the vinegar belonged in the recipe. The intuitive change was small and made the sauce delicious. Mara would liken the process to tuning an instrument if she were not currently chewing a piece of bread with a saucepan in one hand.","Remy watched from the passenger deck, his mice still secure beside his mother. He had made paper chef hats for them. The mother let him show the box from a distance, then returned it to its quiet place. Pets were not edible decorations for a television moment. When the host proposed a closer picture near the grill, she refused. The skipper supported her. The food area stayed separate, and the mice remained undisturbed.","At the bar, a bottle of whiskey had rolled against a stack of glasses. Cassian secured it before the boat changed speed. The prize whisky remained behind the counter, and an unopened brandy was moved with it. The bar steward labelled every beverage containing alcohol and put the liquor bottles safely aside. The ordinary precautions did more for everyone's safety than Flint's dramatic flame. A wine glass broke at another table; a waiter cleared the area promptly so nobody had to stand among shards to look elegant.","A barbecue should have been Flint's comfortable territory. Yet the boat's compact grill was not his studio setup. He overcooked one portion of meat, then blamed the burner. Ess offered a practical adjustment. He waved her off. She kept the bitter reply to herself on that account. Superficially, the performance stayed confident. His skewers did not. A guest began to chew one with a tactful expression that fooled nobody.","Flint’s calm had been superficially convincing, but the contest was intensely competitive. Mara's second course won by two points. Cassian looked so triumphant that she put a finger to his lips before he could make a speech. He kissed it. He remained susceptible to any sign that his wife was pleased. The gesture was sly and utterly ineffective as a protest. She was blushing when the judges asked her to explain the sauce.","The afternoon was pretty much a tie now. Rowan offered beer while guests donated to keep the boat afloat. A little kindness could thereby turn embarrassment into support. Mara chose melon juice instead and stole a piece of chocolate from Cassian's apron pocket. A whopping cheer rose when Ess won a separate people's choice vote for her bean and tomato salad. Flint applauded late. The difference was of small account on the score sheet and considerable account to the young cook.","The energy sector guest had returned with a small donation to the steamer's repair fund. She said a record-high bill should not impoverish a community attraction beyond recovery. The skipper thanked her and declined to promise unlimited trips. He would dedicate the gift to the stated work. From the following month onwards, a repaired hatch would also spare the staff this particular inconvenience.","Remy dropped a paper patriotic flag into the wind. It became briefly airborne before Cassian caught it against his chest. The boy gasped as though he had watched a trapeze act. Mara said she would never hear the end of that rescue. Her husband looked entirely prepared to make this true.","Mara rinsed a lemon, set the honey beside it, and asked Cassian to crush the candy topping. His first attempt sent a fragment into the air. She opposed repeating that experiment. The final round was dessert. The well-orchestrated service left them room to work. No score was unchangeable until the judges finished tasting. Mara was likely to remember Ess’s kindness longer than the result. A cocktail maker offered a fruit cordial to anyone who wanted to pause. Ess brought a crisp biscuit to Mara's station and asked if they could talk after the contest. She had begun to explore a life in which cooking did not mean being humiliated for a living. Mara nodded. The crew’s lunch was ready: ham in one sandwich, cabbage in another. Ess put milk back in the cold box before it warmed.
